Grounding
Pattern Definition: Connecting model outputs to verifiable, real-world information sources.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/tool-bus.ts (lines 1-150)
export class ToolBus {
  async executeTool(toolName: string, args: any): Promise<ToolResult> {
    // Validate tool exists in manifest
    const manifest = this.manifests.get(toolName);
    if (!manifest) throw new Error(`Unknown tool: ${toolName}`);
    
    // Execute against REAL DATA SOURCES
    switch(toolName) {
      case 'get_game_plan':
        return await this.querySQL('SELECT * FROM game_plan WHERE student_id = $1');
      case 'get_awards':
        return await this.querySQL('SELECT * FROM kb_items WHERE type = "award"');
      // ... 105 total resolvers mapping to SQL/KB
    }
  }
}
Match Assessment: ✅ EXCELLENT MATCH - BEST IN CLASS
What's Present: 105 temporal fact resolvers, all SQL-grounded
Evidence: Zero-hallucination architecture, every answer traced to database
Quality: Industry-leading grounding implementation
Why This Is Exemplary:
Complete Coverage: 105 resolvers cover all student data domains
Zero Hallucination: v2.1 tested - 7/7 agents passing hallucination tests
Provenance Tracking: Evidence chips show SQL/RAG/LLM source for every claim
Temporal Precision: Deterministic tie-breakers (deadline_latest: priority → created_at → college_name)
Multi-dimensional: CAT-1 (SQL) → CAT-2 (RAG) → CAT-3 (LLM) fallback hierarchy
Evidence Chip System (v3.2):
// File: backend/src/chips/chip-creator.ts (lines 1-200)
export enum ChipType {
  SQL = 'SQL',        // Direct database facts
  RAG = 'RAG',        // Knowledge Moat (DS6/DS7)
  LLM = 'LLM',        // Model inference
  EQ = 'EQ',          // Empathy/coaching voice
  NARRATIVE = 'NARRATIVE'  // Long-form synthesis
}

interface Evidence {
  chip_type: ChipType;
  confidence: number;      // 0.0-1.0
  source_query?: string;   // SQL query executed
  kb_doc_ids?: string[];   // RAG doc IDs
  model_reasoning?: string; // LLM chain-of-thought
  timestamp: Date;
}
What Makes This World-Class:
Transparency: Every agent response annotated with source type
Auditability: Full provenance trail from user query → SQL → LLM → response
Debuggability: Can trace why agent gave specific answer
Trust: Students/coaches see "grounded in your data" vs "AI inference"

What Could Be Enhanced:
// 1. Real-time Fact Verification
const factChecker = new FactVerificationService({
  crossReference: ['external_apis', 'trusted_sources'],
  confidence: 0.95  // Only output if 95%+ certain
});

// 2. Contradiction Detection

  sources: ['sql_facts', 'kb_items', 'llm_output'],
  resolution: 'prefer_sql'  // SQL wins conflicts

1.2 Core AI Model Architectures
Transformers
Pattern Definition: Self-attention mechanism for processing sequences.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/openai-client.ts (lines 1-50)
import OpenAI from 'openai';

export const openai = new OpenAI({
  apiKey: process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY,
});

export async function callChatCompletion(messages: Message[], tools?: Tool[]) {
  const response = await openai.chat.completions.create({
    model: 'gpt-4-0125-preview',  // Transformer-based
    messages,
    tools,
    temperature: 0.7,
  });
  return response;
}
Match Assessment: ✅ STRONG MATCH (Indirect)
What's Present: Using GPT-4 (transformer architecture) via OpenAI API
Evidence: All 7 agents powered by GPT-4-0125-preview model
Limitation: No direct architectural control (black-box API)
Strengths:
Using state-of-the-art transformer model (GPT-4)
Appropriate model selection for reasoning + tool use
Consistent model across all agents (no mix-match issues)
Weaknesses:
No model customization: Cannot fine-tune attention mechanisms
Black box: Cannot inspect or modify transformer layers
Single vendor: Locked into OpenAI ecosystem
No model versioning strategy: What happens when GPT-4 updates?
Recommendations:
// 1. Model Registry Pattern
const modelRegistry = {
  reasoning: 'gpt-4-0125-preview',      // Complex planning, tool selection
  factual: 'gpt-4-turbo',               // Faster, cheaper for SQL queries
  creative: 'gpt-4-0125-preview',       // Essay feedback, narrative
  embeddings: 'text-embedding-3-large', // RAG, similarity
};

// 2. A/B Testing Infrastructure
const modelTester = new ModelABTest({
  variants: [
    { name: 'gpt-4', model: 'gpt-4-0125-preview' },
    { name: 'claude-3', model: 'claude-3-opus-20240229' },
    { name: 'gemini', model: 'gemini-1.5-pro' }
  ],
  metrics: ['latency', 'cost', 'quality'],
  traffic_split: [70, 20, 10]  // Mostly GPT-4, test alternatives
});

// 3. Fallback Strategy
const modelFallback = new FallbackChain([
  { provider: 'openai', model: 'gpt-4' },
  { provider: 'anthropic', model: 'claude-3-opus' },
  { provider: 'google', model: 'gemini-1.5-pro' }
]);

Mixture of Experts (MoE)
Pattern Definition: Router network selects subset of expert networks dynamically.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// File: backend/src/agents/orchestrator/intent-router.ts (lines 1-150)
export class IntentRouter {
  routeToAgent(userMessage: string): AgentType {
    // Keyword-based routing (NOT true MoE)
    if (message.includes('game plan')) return 'gameplan';
    if (message.includes('college') || message.includes('school')) {
      // Disambiguation: summer programs vs college programs
      if (message.includes('summer') || message.includes('program')) {
        return 'programs';  // Not 'colleges'
      }
      return 'colleges';
    }
    if (message.includes('essay')) return 'essay';
    // ... more rules
  }
}
Match Assessment: ⚠️ PARTIAL MATCH (Conceptual Similarity)
What's Present: Multi-agent routing (7 specialist agents)
How It Works: Rule-based routing, not learned routing
Similarity to MoE: Concept of specialized experts, but missing dynamic router
Comparison:
Aspect
True MoE
IvyLevel Implementation
Router
Neural network
Rule-based (if/else)
Selection
Dynamic, learned
Static, deterministic
Expert Activation
Sparse (1-2 of N)
Single (1 of 7)
Load Balancing
Automatic
Manual rules
Adaptability
Self-improving
Fixed rules

What's Good:
✅ Specialization: Each agent has domain expertise
✅ Efficiency: Only 1 agent activated per query (not all 7)
✅ Modularity: Easy to add new agents
What's Missing:
❌ Learned Routing: No ML model deciding which agent to use
❌ Multi-Agent Queries: Cannot route to 2+ agents simultaneously
❌ Confidence Scoring: Router doesn't express uncertainty
❌ Fallback Logic: If primary agent fails, no secondary routing
Recommendations:
// 1. ML-Based Router (Use Embeddings)
const smartRouter = new EmbeddingRouter({
  model: 'text-embedding-3-large',
  agents: [
    { name: 'gameplan', examples: ['show my timeline', 'what should I do next'] },
    { name: 'colleges', examples: ['college list', 'reach schools', 'safety'] },
    { name: 'essay', examples: ['essay feedback', 'common app prompt'] },
    // ... 7 agents with 10+ examples each
  ],
  threshold: 0.8,  // Route to agent if similarity > 0.8
  fallback: 'gameplan'  // Default if no match
});

// 2. Multi-Agent Routing (MoE-style)
const ensembleRouter = new EnsembleRouter({
  strategy: 'top-k',  // Route to top 2-3 agents
  aggregation: 'voting',  // Combine responses via majority vote
  confidence: true  // Return routing scores
});

// Example: "Tell me about Stanford's essays and deadlines"
// → Routes to [Essay Agent (0.85), Colleges Agent (0.78), Admissions Agent (0.65)]
// → Each responds, ensemble synthesizes final answer

// 3. Dynamic Load Balancing
const loadBalancer = new AgentLoadBalancer({
  metrics: ['queue_depth', 'response_time', 'error_rate'],
  rebalance_interval: 60_000,  // 1 minute
  strategy: 'least-loaded'
});

2.2 Fine-tuning
Pattern Definition: Adapting pre-trained model to specific tasks with small datasets.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// PARTIAL IMPLEMENTATION (Planned, not yet executed)
// File: docs references to DS6/DS7 data (coaching intelligence)

// Knowledge Moat:
// - DS6: 93 weeks of Jenny-Huda coaching sessions
// - DS7: AO perspectives, success patterns
// - DS-T1/DS-T2: Tactics, strategic formulas

// Current: RAG-based retrieval
// Missing: Fine-tuned models on Jenny's coaching style
Match Assessment: ⚠️ SIGNIFICANT GAP
What's Present: RAG system (retrieve coaching knowledge at inference)
What's Missing: Fine-tuned models that embody Jenny's voice natively
Evidence: v2.1 mentions "Jenny's humanizer (voice layer)" but no fine-tuning details
Current Approach (RAG):
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/rag-retriever.ts (conceptual)
async function getCoachingAdvice(query: string) {
  // 1. Embed query
  const queryEmbedding = await embedQuery(query);
  
  // 2. Search KB for relevant docs (DS6/DS7)
  const relevantDocs = await vectorSearch(queryEmbedding, topK=3);
  
  // 3. Inject into prompt
  const prompt = `
    Coaching Context:
    ${relevantDocs.map(d => d.content).join('\n\n')}
    
    User Query: ${query}
    
    Respond in Jenny Duan's coaching style...
  `;
  
  // 4. Call GPT-4 (NOT fine-tuned)
  return await openai.chat.completions.create({ messages: [{ role: 'user', content: prompt }] });
}
What's Missing (Fine-tuning):
// Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) on Jenny's Coaching Data
const finetuneConfig = {
  base_model: 'gpt-4-0125-preview',
  training_data: [
    // 93 weeks × ~5 sessions/week × ~1000 words/session
    // = ~465,000 words of Jenny's coaching conversations
    {
      messages: [
        { role: 'system', content: 'You are Jenny Duan, elite college admissions coach.' },
        { role: 'user', content: 'I\'m worried I\'m not doing enough extracurriculars.' },
        { role: 'assistant', content: '[Jenny\'s actual response from DS6]' }
      ]
    },
    // ... 1000+ examples
  ],
  hyperparameters: {
    n_epochs: 3,
    batch_size: 4,
    learning_rate: 1e-5
  }
};

// Result: GPT-4-Jenny model that natively speaks in her voice
// - No need to inject coaching context every time
// - Faster inference (no RAG retrieval)
// - More consistent voice/style
Benefits of Fine-tuning for IvyLevel:
Voice Consistency: Model inherently speaks like Jenny (not prompt-engineered)
Reduced Latency: No RAG retrieval needed for style (only facts)
Better EQ: Fine-tuned empathy patterns vs generic GPT-4
Competitive Moat: Custom model = IP protection
Recommendation: HIGH PRIORITY
// Phase 1: Data Preparation (2 sprints)

Guardrails
Pattern Definition: Safety layer to filter outputs and block harmful actions.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/guardrails.ts (v3.2)
export class SafetyGuardrails {
  // 1. PII Scrubbing
  async redactPII(text: string): Promise<string> {
    return text
      .replace(/\b[\w.-]+@[\w.-]+\.\w+\b/g, '[EMAIL_REDACTED]')
      .replace(/\b\d{3}-\d{2}-\d{4}\b/g, '[SSN_REDACTED]')
      .replace(/\b\d{3}-\d{3}-\d{4}\b/g, '[PHONE_REDACTED]')
      .replace(/\b\d{1,2}\/\d{1,2}\/\d{4}\b/g, '[DOB_REDACTED]');
  }
  
  // 2. EQ Safety Rails (v3.2)
  async checkEQSafety(response: string, context: string): Promise<boolean> {
    const similarity = await cosineSimilarity(response, context);
    if (similarity < 0.85) {
      logWarning('Low EQ-context similarity', { similarity });
      return false;
    }
    
    // Banned phrases
    const bannedPhrases = [
      'you should give up',
      'you\'re not good enough',
      'this is hopeless'
    ];
    for (const phrase of bannedPhrases) {
      if (response.toLowerCase().includes(phrase)) {
        logError('Banned phrase detected', { phrase });
        return false;
      }
    }
    
    return true;
  }
  
  // 3. Tool Execution Limits
  async checkToolBudget(toolName: string): Promise<boolean> {
    const usage = await this.getToolUsage(toolName);
    const budget = TOOL_BUDGETS[toolName];
    
    if (usage >= budget) {
      throw new Error(`Tool budget exceeded: ${toolName} (${usage}/${budget})`);
    }
    
    return true;
  }
}

3.1 Tool Use
Pattern Definition: Agent utilizes external tools to extend capabilities.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/tool-bus.ts (lines 1-400)
export class ToolBus {
  private tools: Map<string, ToolManifest> = new Map();
  
  // 105 Tools Registered
  registerTool(manifest: ToolManifest) {
    this.tools.set(manifest.name, manifest);
  }
  
  async executeTool(name: string, args: any): Promise<ToolResult> {
    const tool = this.tools.get(name);
    if (!tool) throw new Error(`Unknown tool: ${name}`);
    
    // Validation (Ajv schema)
    const valid = this.validator.validate(tool.parameters_schema, args);
    if (!valid) throw new Error(`Invalid arguments for ${name}`);
    
    // Execute with tracing
    const span = tracer.startSpan(`tool.${name}`);
    try {
      const result = await tool.execute(args);
      span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
      return result;
    } catch (error) {
      span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR, message: error.message });
      throw error;
    } finally {
      span.end();
    }
  }
}

  };

// File: backend/src/agents/game-plan/dynamic-planner.ts (PROPOSED)

interface Plan {
  goal: string;                  // High-level objective
  steps: PlanStep[];             // Ordered sub-goals
  dependencies: Map<number, number[]>;  // Step X requires Step Y complete
  estimated_duration: number;    // Total weeks/days
}

interface PlanStep {
  id: number;
  description: string;
  tools_needed: string[];        // Which tools to call
  success_criteria: string;      // How to know it's done
  estimated_effort: number;      // Hours or days
  status: 'pending' | 'in_progress' | 'completed';
}

class DynamicPlanner {
  async createPlan(goal: string, context: StudentContext): Promise<Plan> {
    // 1. Break down goal into sub-goals (LLM reasoning)
    const decomposition = await this.llm.chat({
      messages: [
        { role: 'system', content: 'You are a planning expert. Break down goals into steps.' },
        { role: 'user', content: `Goal: ${goal}\nContext: ${JSON.stringify(context)}` }
      ]
    });
    
    // 2. For each sub-goal, identify tools needed
    const steps = decomposition.steps.map(step => ({
      ...step,
      tools_needed: this.identifyTools(step.description)
    }));
    
    // 3. Build dependency graph
    const dependencies = this.buildDependencyGraph(steps);
    
    // 4. Estimate durations
    const estimated_duration = steps.reduce((sum, s) => sum + s.estimated_effort, 0);
    
    return { goal, steps, dependencies, estimated_duration };
  }
  
  async executePlan(plan: Plan): Promise<PlanResult> {
    const results = [];
    
    // Execute steps in dependency order (topological sort)
    for (const step of this.topologicalSort(plan.steps, plan.dependencies)) {
      console.log(`Executing step ${step.id}: ${step.description}`);
      
      // Call tools for this step
      for (const toolName of step.tools_needed) {
        const result = await this.toolBus.executeTool(toolName, { student_id: this.studentId });
        results.push({ step: step.id, tool: toolName, result });
      }
      
      // Check success criteria
      const success = await this.checkSuccessCriteria(step, results);
      if (!success) {
        console.warn(`Step ${step.id} failed success criteria, re-planning...`);
        return await this.replan(plan, step);
      }
      
      step.status = 'completed';
    }
    
    return { success: true, results };
  }
}
Use Case Example:
User: "Help me finalize my college list by next week"
         ↓
Dynamic Planner creates plan:
  Step 1: Get current colleges (get_colleges)
  Step 2: Get acceptance stats (get_college_acceptances)
  Step 3: Analyze fit (analyze_college_fit - new tool)
  Step 4: Identify gaps (identify_reach_target_safety_gaps - new tool)
  Step 5: Suggest additions (suggest_colleges_to_add)
  Step 6: Get coach approval (human_in_the_loop)
  Step 7: Finalize list (update_college_list)
         ↓
Execute steps 1-7 in order, checking success after each
         ↓
Return: "Your college list is finalized. Added 3 reach schools (MIT, Stanford, Caltech), removed 2 duplicates."
Benefits of Explicit Planning:
Transparency: Student sees what agent will do before execution
Error Recovery: If step fails, re-plan from that point
Progress Tracking: "Step 2 of 7 complete..."
Complexity Handling: Can handle multi-step requests that current system can't

3.3 Reflection
Pattern Definition: Agent critiques its own work to improve quality.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// NO EXPLICIT IMPLEMENTATION
// No reflection/self-critique pattern in codebase

Gap: Critical for high-quality essay feedback, strategy refinement
What Reflection Would Look Like:
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/reflection-pattern.ts (PROPOSED)

class ReflectionAgent {
  async generateWithReflection(
    task: string,
    maxIterations: number = 3
  ): Promise<string> {
    let output = await this.initialGeneration(task);
    let iteration = 0;
    
    while (iteration < maxIterations) {
      iteration++;
      
      // 1. Self-Critique
      const critique = await this.critique(output, task);
      
      // 2. If no issues found, return
      if (critique.issues.length === 0) {
        console.log(`Reflection complete after ${iteration} iterations`);
        return output;
      }
      
      // 3. Otherwise, improve based on critique
      console.log(`Iteration ${iteration}: Found ${critique.issues.length} issues`);
      output = await this.improve(output, critique);
    }
    
    console.log(`Max iterations reached (${maxIterations})`);
    return output;
  }
  
  private async critique(output: string, task: string): Promise<Critique> {
    const response = await this.llm.chat({
      messages: [
        {
          role: 'system',
          content: `You are a critic. Review this output and identify:
            1. Factual errors
            2. Missing information
            3. Unclear explanations
            4. Tone issues
            5. Formatting problems
            
            If output is excellent, return empty issues array.`
        },
        {
          role: 'user',
          content: `Task: ${task}\n\nOutput:\n${output}\n\nProvide critique as JSON.`
        }
      ]
    });
    
    return JSON.parse(response);
  }
  
  private async improve(output: string, critique: Critique): Promise<string> {
    const response = await this.llm.chat({
      messages: [
        {
          role: 'system',
          content: 'Revise the output to address the following issues:'
        },
        {
          role: 'user',
          content: `Original Output:\n${output}\n\nIssues:\n${critique.issues.join('\n')}\n\nRevised Output:`
        }
      ]
    });
    
    return response;
  }
}
Use Cases Where Reflection Is Critical:
1. Essay Feedback (Essay Agent)
Current: Single-pass feedback
  User: "Review my Common App essay"
  Essay Agent: [generates feedback in one shot]
  Problem: May miss nuanced issues

Reflection: Multi-iteration feedback
  User: "Review my Common App essay"
  Essay Agent:
    Iteration 1: Draft feedback
    Critique: "Feedback is too harsh, lacks constructive examples"
    Iteration 2: Improved feedback (softer tone + examples)
    Critique: "Good! No issues found."
  Result: Higher-quality, empathetic feedback
2. College List Strategy (Colleges Agent)
Current: Single recommendation
  User: "Suggest 3 reach schools"
  Colleges Agent: MIT, Stanford, Caltech
  Problem: Doesn't verify fit (geography, major, culture)

Reflection: Validated recommendations
  User: "Suggest 3 reach schools"
  Colleges Agent:
    Iteration 1: MIT, Stanford, Caltech
    Critique: "Student wants small liberal arts colleges, these are large research universities"
    Iteration 2: Williams, Amherst, Swarthmore
    Critique: "Good fit! All are small, strong CS programs, East Coast."
  Result: Better personalized recommendations
3. Game Plan Timeline (Game Plan Agent)
Current: Static timeline
  User: "Build my junior year timeline"
  Game Plan Agent: [returns pre-computed milestones]
  Problem: Doesn't account for student's unique situation

Reflection: Personalized timeline
  User: "Build my junior year timeline"
  Game Plan Agent:
    Iteration 1: Standard timeline (SAT, APs, ECs)
    Critique: "Student already has 1530 SAT, doesn't need more test prep"
    Iteration 2: Revised timeline (focus on ECs + essays, remove SAT prep)
    Critique: "Great! Aligns with student's strengths."
  Result: Customized, efficient timeline

Recommendation: HIGH PRIORITY

Debuggability: Can identify flawed reasoning (e.g., wrong priority calculation)
Research Evidence:
Wei et al. (2022): "Chain of Thought Prompting Improves Reasoning in Large Language Models"
CoT improves accuracy by 10-30% on complex reasoning tasks
Particularly effective for multi-step math, logic, strategy problems
Use Cases:
1. Timeline Optimization (Game Plan Agent)
Without CoT: "Do X, Y, Z" (no explanation)
With CoT: "Given your SAT next week [reasoning], prioritize X over Y [explanation], then Z [rationale]"
2. College Fit Analysis (Colleges Agent)
Without CoT: "You should apply to MIT, Stanford, Caltech"
With CoT: "Analyzing fit... MIT: strong CS + robotics [match], but very competitive [risk]. Stanford: excellent entrepreneurship programs [match], good culture fit [personality]. Caltech: top STEM [match], but small + intense [culture mismatch]. Recommendation: Apply MIT + Stanford, reconsider Caltech."
3. Essay Strategy (Essay Agent)
Without CoT: "Focus on your robotics project"
With CoT: "Essay topics analysis: Robotics (interesting but common in CS applicants), immigrant parents (authentic but not unique), overcoming dyslexia (demonstrates grit, less common, personal growth). Recommendation: Lead with dyslexia journey, weave in robotics as example of perseverance."
Implementation:
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/cot-wrapper.ts (PROPOSED)

class ChainOfThoughtWrapper {
  wrapPrompt(originalPrompt: string): string {
    return `${originalPrompt}

CRITICAL INSTRUCTION:
Before providing your final answer, show your reasoning step-by-step.
Use this format:

REASONING:
<Your detailed thought process>
- Consider all relevant factors
- Analyze trade-offs
- Explain why you're recommending X over Y

ANSWER:
<Your final recommendation>
`;
  }
  
  async generateWithCoT(query: string): Promise<CoTResponse> {
    const response = await this.llm.chat({
      messages: [
        { role: 'system', content: this.wrapPrompt(this.systemPrompt) },
        { role: 'user', content: query }
      ]
    });
    
    // Parse response into reasoning + answer
    const [reasoning, answer] = response.split('ANSWER:');
    
    return {
      reasoning: reasoning.replace('REASONING:', '').trim(),
      answer: answer.trim(),
      full_response: response
    };
  }
}

3.5 Tree of Thoughts (ToT)
Pattern Definition: Explore multiple reasoning paths simultaneously, evaluate each.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// NO IMPLEMENTATION
Match Assessment: ❌ NOT IMPLEMENTED
What's Present: Zero Tree of Thoughts mechanisms
Evidence: No branching reasoning, no path evaluation
Gap: Useful for complex strategic decisions (college list strategy, essay topic selection)
What Tree of Thoughts Would Look Like:
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/tree-of-thoughts.ts (PROPOSED)

interface ThoughtNode {
  id: string;
  content: string;           // The thought/reasoning step
  parent_id?: string;        // Which thought led to this
  score: number;             // Quality evaluation (0-1)
  children: ThoughtNode[];   // Branching paths
}

class TreeOfThoughts {
  async solve(
    problem: string,
    branching_factor: number = 3,  // Explore 3 paths per node
    depth: number = 3               // Max depth of tree
  ): Promise<ThoughtNode> {
    // 1. Generate initial thoughts
    const root = await this.generateThoughts(problem, branching_factor);
    
    // 2. Build tree by expanding best nodes
    let currentLevel = [root];
    for (let d = 0; d < depth; d++) {
      const nextLevel = [];
      
      for (const node of currentLevel) {
        // Expand each node (generate child thoughts)
        node.children = await this.generateThoughts(node.content, branching_factor);
        
        // Evaluate each child
        for (const child of node.children) {
          child.score = await this.evaluate(child.content, problem);
        }
        
        // Prune: Keep only top 2 children per node
        node.children = node.children
          .sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score)
          .slice(0, 2);
        
        nextLevel.push(...node.children);
      }
      
      currentLevel = nextLevel;
    }
    
    // 3. Select best path (highest-scoring leaf → root)
    const bestLeaf = this.findBestLeaf(root);
    const bestPath = this.tracePathToRoot(bestLeaf);
    
    return bestPath;
  }
  
  private async generateThoughts(
    context: string,
    n: number
  ): Promise<ThoughtNode[]> {
    const response = await this.llm.chat({
      messages: [
        {
          role: 'system',
          content: `Generate ${n} different reasoning paths for this problem.
                    Each should take a different strategic approach.`
        },
        { role: 'user', content: context }
      ]
    });
    
    // Parse response into multiple thought nodes
    return response.split('\n\n').map((thought, i) => ({
      id: `thought-${i}`,
      content: thought,
      score: 0,
      children: []
    }));
  }
  
  private async evaluate(thought: string, problem: string): Promise<number> {
    const response = await this.llm.chat({
      messages: [
        {
          role: 'system',
          content: 'Rate this reasoning step from 0-1 (0=poor, 1=excellent).'
        },
        {
          role: 'user',
          content: `Problem: ${problem}\nReasoning: ${thought}`
        }
      ]
    });
    
    return parseFloat(response);
  }
}
Use Case: College List Strategy
Problem: "Build optimal college list for CS major with 1530 SAT, 4.0 GPA, strong robotics"

Tree of Thoughts explores 3 strategies in parallel:

Strategy A: "Prestige-focused" (MIT, Stanford, CMU, Berkeley)
  → Branching: A1 (all reaches), A2 (balanced reach/target), A3 (add Ivies)
  → Evaluation: A2 scores highest (0.85) - balanced is realistic
  
Strategy B: "Fit-focused" (smaller CS programs, research opportunities)
  → Branching: B1 (liberal arts CS), B2 (tech-focused), B3 (co-op programs)
  → Evaluation: B2 scores highest (0.80) - tech focus aligns with robotics
  
Strategy C: "Geographic diversity" (East/West/Midwest balance)
  → Branching: C1 (coast-heavy), C2 (midwest-heavy), C3 (balanced)
  → Evaluation: C3 scores highest (0.75) - but lower than A2/B2

Final recommendation: Combine Strategy A2 + Strategy B2
- Reach: MIT, Stanford, CMU (prestige + fit)
- Target: Georgia Tech, UIUC, UT Austin (strong tech programs)
- Safety: UMass Amherst, Purdue, Virginia Tech (solid CS + robotics)
When to Use ToT vs CoT:
Scenario
Use CoT
Use ToT
Single clear path
✅ (fast, simple)
❌ (overkill)
Multiple strategies
⚠️ (picks one)
✅ (explores all)
High-stakes decision
⚠️ (might miss better path)
✅ (thorough)
Time-sensitive
✅ (1 LLM call)
❌ (10+ LLM calls)
Cost-sensitive
✅ (cheap)
❌ (expensive)

Recommendation: MEDIUM PRIORITY (Niche Use Cases)

3.6 ReAct (Reason and Act)
Pattern Definition: Interleave reasoning, acting (tool use), and observing results.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// PARTIAL IMPLEMENTATION (Tool loop, but no explicit reasoning)

// File: backend/src/agents/game-plan/agent.ts (lines 50-150)
async function runGamePlanAgent(userMessage: string): Promise<string> {
  let messages = [
    { role: 'system', content: systemPrompt },
    { role: 'user', content: userMessage }
  ];
  
  let iterations = 0;
  const MAX_ITERATIONS = 5;
  
  while (iterations < MAX_ITERATIONS) {
    iterations++;
    
    // 1. Call LLM (Reasoning + Action decision)
    const response = await openai.chat.completions.create({
      model: 'gpt-4-0125-preview',
      messages,
      tools: gameplanTools,  // List of available tools
    });
    
    // 2. Check if agent wants to use tool (Action)
    if (response.tool_calls) {
      for (const toolCall of response.tool_calls) {
        // 3. Execute tool (Observe)
        const toolResult = await toolBus.executeTool(
          toolCall.function.name,
          JSON.parse(toolCall.function.arguments)
        );
        
        // 4. Add observation to messages
        messages.push({
          role: 'tool',
          tool_call_id: toolCall.id,
          content: JSON.stringify(toolResult)
        });
      }
      
      // Loop continues: LLM sees tool result, reasons about it, may call more tools
      continue;
    }
    
    // 5. No more tools needed, return final answer
    return response.choices[0].message.content;
  }
  
  throw new Error('Max iterations reached');
}
Match Assessment: ⚠️ PARTIAL MATCH (50%)
What's Present: Tool loop (Action + Observe)
What's Missing: Explicit reasoning steps (Thought)
Evidence: Agent calls tools, but doesn't verbalize reasoning
Current Flow:
User: "What should I do next?"
         ↓
Agent: [thinks internally, calls get_game_plan]
         ↓
Tool: Returns { current_phase: "Decision", priorities: [...] }
         ↓
Agent: [thinks internally, generates response]
         ↓
Agent: "You should complete your essays..."
True ReAct Flow:
User: "What should I do next?"
         ↓
Agent: Thought: "I need to check the student's current game plan status."
       Action: get_game_plan(student_id=123)
         ↓
Tool: Returns { current_phase: "Decision", priorities: [...] }
         ↓
Agent: Observation: "Student is in Decision phase with 12 weeks left. Essays are 3/5 complete."
       Thought: "Essays are the highest priority since they're partially done but not finished."
       Action: get_essay_status(student_id=123)
         ↓
Tool: Returns { common_app: "complete", supplements: [MIT (draft), Stanford (not started)] }
         ↓
Agent: Observation: "Stanford supplement hasn't been started yet, MIT is in draft."
       Thought: "Stanford should be next priority since it's completely missing."
       Answer: "You should complete your Stanford supplement next. It's due in 3 weeks and you haven't started yet. After that, polish your MIT draft."
Benefits of Explicit ReAct:
Transparency: User sees agent's thought process
Debuggability: Can identify where reasoning went wrong
Reliability: Verbalizing thoughts reduces errors (research-proven)
Auditability: Coach can review agent's decision-making
Implementation:
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/react-pattern.ts (PROPOSED)

interface ReActStep {
  type: 'thought' | 'action' | 'observation';
  content: string;
  timestamp: Date;
}

class ReActAgent {
  private trajectory: ReActStep[] = [];  // Full reasoning trace
  
  async run(userMessage: string): Promise<string> {
    let messages = [
      { role: 'system', content: this.systemPromptWithReAct() },
      { role: 'user', content: userMessage }
    ];
    
    let iterations = 0;
    const MAX_ITERATIONS = 5;
    
    while (iterations < MAX_ITERATIONS) {
      iterations++;
      
      // 1. Thought: Ask agent to reason
      const response = await this.llm.chat({ messages });
      
      // 2. Parse response for Thought/Action/Answer
      const parsed = this.parseReActResponse(response);
      
      if (parsed.type === 'thought') {
        this.trajectory.push({
          type: 'thought',
          content: parsed.content,
          timestamp: new Date()
        });
        
        // Continue to next iteration (agent will call action or answer)
        messages.push({ role: 'assistant', content: response });
        continue;
      }
      
      if (parsed.type === 'action') {
        this.trajectory.push({
          type: 'action',
          content: `${parsed.tool_name}(${JSON.stringify(parsed.args)})`,
          timestamp: new Date()
        });
        
        // 3. Execute tool
        const result = await this.toolBus.executeTool(parsed.tool_name, parsed.args);
        
        // 4. Observation: Show tool result to agent
        const observation = `Observation: ${JSON.stringify(result)}`;
        this.trajectory.push({
          type: 'observation',
          content: observation,
          timestamp: new Date()
        });
        
        messages.push({ role: 'assistant', content: response });
        messages.push({ role: 'system', content: observation });
        continue;
      }
      
      if (parsed.type === 'answer') {
        // Agent is done reasoning, return final answer
        return parsed.content;
      }
    }
    
    throw new Error('Max iterations reached');
  }
  
  private systemPromptWithReAct(): string {
    return `You are an agent that uses the ReAct pattern: Reason, Act, Observe.

CRITICAL: Always show your reasoning explicitly.

You have access to these tools:
${this.toolsDescription}

Format:
Thought: <Your reasoning about what to do next>
Action: <tool_name> <args as JSON>
[System returns observation]
Thought: <Your reasoning about the observation>
Action: <next tool> OR Answer: <final response>

Example:
Thought: I need to check the student's game plan to understand their current priorities.
Action: get_game_plan {"student_id": 123}
Observation: {"current_phase": "Decision", "priorities": ["essays", "college list"]}
Thought: Essays are the top priority. I should get details on their essay status.
Action: get_essay_status {"student_id": 123}
Observation: {"common_app": "complete", "supplements": [{"name": "MIT", "status": "draft"}]}
Thought: The MIT supplement is in draft and needs to be finished. That should be the next action.
Answer: You should focus on completing your MIT supplement. It's currently in draft and needs to be polished before the deadline.
`;
  }
  
  private parseReActResponse(response: string): ParsedStep {
    // Extract Thought/Action/Answer from response
    if (response.startsWith('Thought:')) {
      return { type: 'thought', content: response.replace('Thought:', '').trim() };
    }
    if (response.startsWith('Action:')) {
      const [toolName, argsStr] = response.replace('Action:', '').trim().split(' ', 2);
      return {
        type: 'action',
        tool_name: toolName,
        args: JSON.parse(argsStr)
      };
    }
    if (response.startsWith('Answer:')) {
      return { type: 'answer', content: response.replace('Answer:', '').trim() };
    }
    throw new Error('Invalid ReAct format');
  }
  
  getTrajectory(): ReActStep[] {
    return this.trajectory;
  }
}

3.7 Multi-Agent Collaboration
Pattern Definition: Multiple specialized agents work together to solve problems.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// File: backend/src/agents/orchestrator/orchestrator.ts (lines 1-300)

const AGENTS = {
  gameplan: GamePlanAgent,
  colleges: CollegesAgent,
  essay: EssayAgent,
  admissions: AdmissionsAgent,
  ecs: ECAgent,
  awards: AwardsAgent,
  programs: ProgramsAgent
};

class Orchestrator {
  async route(userMessage: string, context: Context): Promise<AgentResponse> {
    // 1. Intent Detection
    const agentName = this.intentRouter.route(userMessage);
    
    // 2. Select Agent
    const agent = AGENTS[agentName];
    
    // 3. Execute Agent
    const response = await agent.run(userMessage, context);
    
    // 4. Return Response
    return {
      agent: agentName,
      response,
      confidence: 0.9  // Placeholder
    };
  }
}

  strategy: 'parallel',  // Execute multiple agents simultaneously
  aggregation: 'synthesis'  // Combine responses intelligently
});

// 2. Agent-to-Agent Handoffs
class HandoffOrchestrator {
  async handleHandoff(
    fromAgent: string,
    toAgent: string,
    context: HandoffContext
  ): Promise<void> {
    console.log(`Handing off from ${fromAgent} to ${toAgent}`);
    
    // Pass context (conversation, data, reasoning) to next agent
    await AGENTS[toAgent].receiveHandoff({
      from: fromAgent,
      conversation_id: context.conversation_id,
      student_id: context.student_id,
      previous_findings: context.data,
      reason: context.handoff_reason
    });
  }
}

// Example: Game Plan Agent → Essay Agent
// "You should work on essays" (Game Plan insight)
//   ↓ handoff ↓
// "Let's brainstorm essay topics" (Essay Agent takes over)

// 3. Collaborative Problem Solving (Debate Pattern)
class DebateOrchestrator {
  async solve(problem: string): Promise<Solution> {
    // 1. Multiple agents propose solutions
    const proposals = await Promise.all([
      this.gameplanAgent.proposeSolution(problem),
      this.collegesAgent.proposeSolution(problem),
      this.admissionsAgent.proposeSolution(problem)
    ]);
    
    // 2. Agents debate/critique each other's proposals
    const critiques = await Promise.all(
      proposals.map(p => this.critiqueAgent.critique(p, problem))
    );
    
    // 3. Agents refine based on critiques
    const refinedProposals = await Promise.all(
      proposals.map((p, i) => p.agent.refine(p.solution, critiques[i]))
    );
    
    // 4. Vote on best solution
    const votes = await this.votingAgent.selectBest(refinedProposals);
    
    return votes[0];  // Highest-voted solution
  }
}

// 4. Dynamic Agent Spawning
class DynamicAgentFactory {
  async spawnTemporaryAgent(
    role: string,
    task: string,
    tools: Tool[]
  ): Promise<Agent> {
    // Create ad-hoc agent for specific task
    const agent = new Agent({
      name: `temp-${role}-${Date.now()}`,
      role,
      system_prompt: `You are a ${role}. Your task: ${task}`,
      tools,
      lifespan: '1 hour'  // Auto-destroy after use
    });
    
    return agent;
  }
}

// Example: Spawn "Financial Aid Analyst" agent on-demand
const finaidAgent = await factory.spawnTemporaryAgent(
  'financial-aid-analyst',
  'Calculate expected family contribution and suggest schools with good aid',
  [get_financial_data, search_college_aid, calculate_efc]
);

// 5. Agent Performance Dashboard
interface AgentMetrics {
  agent_name: string;
  total_queries: number;
  avg_latency: number;        // ms
  success_rate: number;       // 0-1
  tool_calls_per_query: number;
  cost_per_query: number;     // USD
  user_satisfaction: number;  // 1-5 stars
}

class AgentMonitor {
  async getMetrics(): Promise<AgentMetrics[]> {
    return await db.query(`
      SELECT
        agent_name,
        COUNT(*) as total_queries,
        AVG(latency_ms) as avg_latency,
        AVG(CASE WHEN status='success' THEN 1.0 ELSE 0.0 END) as success_rate,
        AVG(tool_calls) as tool_calls_per_query,
        AVG(cost_usd) as cost_per_query,
        AVG(user_rating) as user_satisfaction
      FROM agent_telemetry
      WHERE timestamp > NOW() - INTERVAL '7 days'
      GROUP BY agent_name
    `);
  }
}

3.8 Human-in-the-Loop (HITL)
Pattern Definition: Integrate human oversight for approval, feedback, corrections.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// IMPLICIT IMPLEMENTATION (No explicit HITL pattern)

// File: frontend/src/components/StudentDashboard.tsx (lines 100-300)
// Current: Human views agent responses AFTER execution
// - Coach can review conversation history
// - No approval gates before agent actions
// - No correction mechanisms

// v13.0: Assessment tab allows coach to review Ivy+ scores
// v11.0: Preparation tab shows weekly action plans (static, not agent-generated)
Match Assessment: ⚠️ PARTIAL MATCH (30%)
What's Present: Conversation history review (post-hoc)
What's Missing: Pre-action approval, real-time corrections, feedback loops
Evidence: No explicit HITL checkpoints in agent workflows
Current Interaction Model:
User Query → Agent → Response → User Sees Result
                                   ↓
                           (Coach can review later)
True HITL Model:
User Query → Agent → Proposed Action → HUMAN APPROVAL → Execute → Response
                                              ↓
                                      [Reject] → Agent revises
Where HITL Should Be Implemented:
1. College List Changes
// Current: Agent directly recommends colleges
User: "Suggest 3 reach schools"
Agent: "MIT, Stanford, Caltech"  // No approval needed

// HITL: Agent proposes, coach approves
User: "Suggest 3 reach schools"
Agent: "Proposing: MIT, Stanford, Caltech"
System: [Notification to coach Jenny]
Coach: [Reviews, approves or modifies]
Agent: "Coach approved: MIT, Stanford. Changed Caltech → CMU (better culture fit)"
2. Essay Feedback
// Current: Agent gives feedback directly
User: "Review my Common App essay"
Agent: [critique] "Your opening is weak, consider starting with..."

// HITL: Coach reviews agent's feedback before sending
User: "Review my Common App essay"
Agent: [Generates draft feedback]
System: [Shows draft to coach]
Coach: [Edits: "Too harsh, soften the critique"]
Agent: [Revised feedback] "Your opening has potential. Let's strengthen it by..."
3. Timeline Changes
// Current: Game Plan Agent suggests timeline (but it's pre-computed)
User: "Should I retake the SAT?"
Agent: "Yes, schedule for October"

// HITL: Major timeline decisions require approval
User: "Should I retake the SAT?"
Agent: "Analysis: Current 1530, target 1550. Recommend retake."
System: [Flags as major decision, notifies coach]
Coach: [Reviews student profile, current stress level]
Coach: [Overrides] "No, focus on essays instead. SAT is strong enough."
Agent: "After discussing with your coach, we recommend focusing on essays..."
Implementation:
// File: backend/src/hitl/approval-gate.ts (PROPOSED)

interface ApprovalRequest {
  id: string;
  student_id: number;
  agent_name: string;
  action_type: 'college_change' | 'timeline_shift' | 'major_feedback';
  proposed_action: any;
  rationale: string;
  urgency: 'low' | 'medium' | 'high';
  created_at: Date;
  status: 'pending' | 'approved' | 'rejected' | 'modified';
}

class ApprovalGate {
  async requestApproval(
    action: ProposedAction,
    student_id: number
  ): Promise<ApprovalResult> {
    // 1. Classify action (does it need approval?)
    const needsApproval = this.classifyAction(action);
    if (!needsApproval) {
      return { approved: true, reason: 'low-risk-action' };
    }
    
    // 2. Create approval request
    const request = await db.insert('approval_requests', {
      student_id,
      agent_name: action.agent,
      action_type: action.type,
      proposed_action: action.details,
      rationale: action.reasoning,
      urgency: action.urgency,
      status: 'pending'
    });
    
    // 3. Notify coach (email, SMS, dashboard notification)
    await this.notifyCoach(request);
    
    // 4. Wait for approval (with timeout)
    const result = await this.waitForApproval(request.id, timeout=3600); // 1 hour
    
    // 5. Return result
    return result;
  }
  
  private classifyAction(action: ProposedAction): boolean {
    // Define which actions need approval
    const requiresApproval = [
      'add_reach_college',       // Adding reach schools (risky)
      'remove_safety_college',   // Removing safety (dangerous)
      'change_major',            // Major pivot (big decision)
      'delay_deadline',          // Timeline slip (coach should know)
      'essay_harsh_critique'     // Negative feedback (check tone)
    ];
    
    return requiresApproval.includes(action.type);
  }
  
  async waitForApproval(
    requestId: string,
    timeout: number
  ): Promise<ApprovalResult> {
    const startTime = Date.now();
    
    while (Date.now() - startTime < timeout * 1000) {
      const request = await db.get('approval_requests', requestId);
      
      if (request.status === 'approved') {
        return { approved: true, modified_action: request.modified_action };
      }
      
      if (request.status === 'rejected') {
        return { approved: false, reason: request.rejection_reason };
      }
      
      if (request.status === 'modified') {
        return { approved: true, modified_action: request.modified_action };
      }
      
      // Wait 5 seconds before checking again
      await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 5000));
    }
    
    // Timeout: default to safe action (usually reject)
    return { approved: false, reason: 'timeout' };
  }
}
Coach Dashboard:
// File: frontend/src/components/CoachDashboard.tsx (PROPOSED)

function ApprovalQueue() {
  const [requests, setRequests] = useState<ApprovalRequest[]>([]);
  
  useEffect(() => {
    // Poll for pending approvals
    const interval = setInterval(async () => {
      const pending = await api.get('/approvals/pending');
      setRequests(pending);
    }, 10_000);  // Every 10 seconds
    
    return () => clearInterval(interval);
  }, []);
  
  async function approve(requestId: string) {
    await api.post(`/approvals/${requestId}/approve`);
    setRequests(prev => prev.filter(r => r.id !== requestId));
  }
  
  async function reject(requestId: string, reason: string) {
    await api.post(`/approvals/${requestId}/reject`, { reason });
    setRequests(prev => prev.filter(r => r.id !== requestId));
  }
  
  async function modify(requestId: string, modifiedAction: any) {
    await api.post(`/approvals/${requestId}/modify`, { modifiedAction });
    setRequests(prev => prev.filter(r => r.id !== requestId));
  }
  
  return (
    <div className="approval-queue">
      <h2>Pending Approvals ({requests.length})</h2>
      {requests.map(req => (
        <ApprovalCard
          key={req.id}
          request={req}
          onApprove={() => approve(req.id)}
          onReject={(reason) => reject(req.id, reason)}
          onModify={(action) => modify(req.id, action)}
        />
      ))}
    </div>
  );
}

3.9 Memory Management
Pattern Definition: Maintain context across conversations, sessions, time.
IvyLevel Implementation:
// File: backend/src/agents/shared/memory/memory-service.ts (v3.2)

// 1. Session Memory (Short-term, per conversation)
class InMemorySessionService {
  private sessions: Map<string, ConversationSession> = new Map();
  
  async getSession(sessionId: string): Promise<ConversationSession> {
    return this.sessions.get(sessionId) || this.createNewSession(sessionId);
  }
  
  async addMessage(sessionId: string, message: Message): Promise<void> {
    const session = await this.getSession(sessionId);
    session.messages.push(message);
    this.sessions.set(sessionId, session);
  }
}

// 2. Database Session (Long-term, persistent)
class DatabaseSessionService {
  async getSession(sessionId: string): Promise<ConversationSession> {
    return await db.query(`
      SELECT * FROM conversations
      WHERE session_id = $1
      ORDER BY created_at ASC
    `, [sessionId]);
  }
  
  async addMessage(sessionId: string, message: Message): Promise<void> {
    await db.insert('conversations', {
      session_id: sessionId,
      student_id: message.student_id,
      coach_id: message.coach_id,
      role: message.role,
      content: message.content,
      agent_name: message.agent_name,
      created_at: new Date()
    });
  }
}

// 3. Episodic Memory (Key events, milestones)
class EpisodicMemoryService {
  async recordEvent(event: GrowthEvent): Promise<void> {
    await db.insert('growth_events', {
      student_id: event.student_id,
      event_type: event.type,  // 'breakthrough', 'milestone', 'setback'
      description: event.description,
      metadata: event.metadata,
      occurred_at: event.occurred_at,
      created_at: new Date()
    });
  }
  
  async getKeyEvents(studentId: number, limit: number = 10): Promise<GrowthEvent[]> {
    return await db.query(`
      SELECT * FROM growth_events
      WHERE student_id = $1
      ORDER BY occurred_at DESC
      LIMIT $2
    `, [studentId, limit]);
  }
}

// 4. Semantic Memory (Long-term knowledge, facts)
class SemanticMemoryService {
  async storeFact(fact: StudentFact): Promise<void> {
    await db.insert('kb_items', {
      student_id: fact.student_id,
      type: fact.type,  // 'award', 'test_score', 'ec_activity'
      data: fact.data,
      source: fact.source,
      confidence: fact.confidence,
      created_at: new Date()
    });
  }
  
  async recallFacts(studentId: number, type?: string): Promise<StudentFact[]> {
    let query = `SELECT * FROM kb_items WHERE student_id = $1`;
    const params = [studentId];
    
    if (type) {
      query += ` AND type = $2`;
      params.push(type);
    }
    
    return await db.query(query, params);
  }
}

// Proposed: Procedural Memory
interface ProceduralMemory {
  pattern: string;           // "Recommend colleges"
  context: any;              // { student_profile: { SAT: 1500, ... } }
  action: string;            // "Suggested MIT, Stanford"
  outcome: string;           // "Student rejected, wanted smaller schools"
  learned_rule: string;      // "For students preferring small schools, avoid large research universities"
  confidence: number;        // 0-1
}

class ProceduralMemoryService {
  async recordLearning(memory: ProceduralMemory): Promise<void> {
    await db.insert('procedural_memory', memory);
  }
  
  async recall(pattern: string, context: any): Promise<ProceduralMemory[]> {
    // Retrieve similar past situations
    const similar = await db.query(`
      SELECT * FROM procedural_memory
      WHERE pattern = $1
      AND similarity(context, $2) > 0.8  -- Cosine similarity
      ORDER BY confidence DESC
      LIMIT 5
    `, [pattern, context]);
    
    return similar;
  }
}
2. Emotional Context (EQ Memory)
// Current: Events are recorded, but not emotional state

// Proposed: Emotional Context
interface EmotionalMemory {
  student_id: number;
  timestamp: Date;
  emotion: 'stressed' | 'excited' | 'frustrated' | 'confident' | 'overwhelmed';
  intensity: number;         // 1-10
  trigger: string;           // "Essay deadline approaching"
  agent_response: string;    // How agent should adapt
}

class EmotionalMemoryService {
  async recordEmotion(emotion: EmotionalMemory): Promise<void> {
    await db.insert('emotional_memory', emotion);
  }
  
  async getRecentEmotion(studentId: number): Promise<EmotionalMemory | null> {
    // Get student's emotional state from last 24 hours
    return await db.query(`
      SELECT * FROM emotional_memory
      WHERE student_id = $1
      AND timestamp > NOW() - INTERVAL '24 hours'
      ORDER BY timestamp DESC
      LIMIT 1
    `, [studentId]);
  }
  
  async adaptToEmotion(emotion: EmotionalMemory): Promise<AgentBehavior> {
    // Adjust agent tone/approach based on emotion
    if (emotion.emotion === 'stressed' && emotion.intensity > 7) {
      return {
        tone: 'extra_empathetic',
        pacing: 'slow_down',
        suggestions: 'break_tasks_into_smaller_steps'
      };
    }
    
    if (emotion.emotion === 'confident') {
      return {
        tone: 'encouraging',
        pacing: 'normal',
        suggestions: 'suggest_stretch_goals'
      };
    }
    
    return { tone: 'default', pacing: 'normal', suggestions: 'standard' };
  }
}
3. Causal Relationships (Why Events Happened)
// Current: Events are recorded, but not cause-effect chains

// Proposed: Causal Memory
interface CausalRelationship {
  cause: Event;              // "Student worked with robotics mentor"
  effect: Event;             // "Won ISEF award"
  confidence: number;        // 0-1 (how sure we are about causation)
  explanation: string;       // "Mentor provided guidance on project design"
}

class CausalMemoryService {
  async recordCausation(causal: CausalRelationship): Promise<void> {
    await db.insert('causal_relationships', causal);
  }
  
  async inferSuccess(goal: string, studentProfile: any): Promise<Action[]> {
    // Find causal chains that led to similar goals
    const successPaths = await db.query(`
      SELECT * FROM causal_relationships
      WHERE effect_type = $1
      AND student_similarity(cause_context, $2) > 0.7
      ORDER BY confidence DESC
      LIMIT 10
    `, [goal, studentProfile]);
    
    // Return recommended actions based on what worked for similar students
    return successPaths.map(p => p.cause.action);
  }
}
4. Working Memory (Attention Mechanism)
// Current: All context is passed to LLM equally

// Proposed: Working Memory with Attention
class WorkingMemoryService {
  async selectRelevantContext(
    query: string,
    fullContext: any[],
    maxTokens: number = 4000
  ): Promise<any[]> {
    // 1. Compute relevance scores
    const scored = await Promise.all(
      fullContext.map(async (item) => ({
        item,
        score: await this.relevanceScore(query, item)
      }))
    );
    
    // 2. Sort by relevance
    scored.sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score);
    
    // 3. Select top items until token budget exhausted
    const selected = [];
    let tokenCount = 0;
    
    for (const { item, score } of scored) {
      const itemTokens = this.estimateTokens(item);
      if (tokenCount + itemTokens > maxTokens) break;
      
      selected.push(item);
      tokenCount += itemTokens;
    }
    
    return selected;
  }
  
  private async relevanceScore(query: string, item: any): Promise<number> {
    // Use embeddings to compute cosine similarity
    const queryEmbed = await this.embed(query);
    const itemEmbed = await this.embed(JSON.stringify(item));
    return cosineSimilarity(queryEmbed, itemEmbed);
  }
}
Memory Consolidation (Daily Batch Process)
// Proposed: Nightly memory consolidation
class MemoryConsolidator {
  async consolidate(studentId: number): Promise<void> {
    // 1. Summarize today's conversations
    const todayChats = await db.query(`
      SELECT * FROM conversations
      WHERE student_id = $1
      AND created_at > NOW() - INTERVAL '24 hours'
    `, [studentId]);
    
    const summary = await this.llm.summarize(todayChats);
    
    // 2. Extract key facts
    const facts = await this.llm.extractFacts(summary);
    for (const fact of facts) {
      await this.semanticMemory.storeFact(fact);
    }
    
    // 3. Identify growth events
    const events = await this.llm.extractEvents(summary);
    for (const event of events) {
      await this.episodicMemory.recordEvent(event);
    }
    
    // 4. Update causal relationships
    const causals = await this.inferCausation(todayChats);
    for (const causal of causals) {
      await this.causalMemory.recordCausation(causal);
    }
    
    // 5. Archive old session memory (move to cold storage)
    await this.archiveOldSessions(studentId, days=30);
  }
}

const systemPrompt = `You are Jenny Duan, elite college admissions coach.

COACHING STYLE EXAMPLES:

Example 1 (Empathy):
Student: "I'm worried I'm not good enough"
Jenny: "I hear you. That feeling is totally normal - even students who get into top schools feel this way. Let's look at your strengths together. You have a 1530 SAT and you've built an incredible robotics project that won ISEF. These are real achievements that show your capability."

Example 2 (Strategic):
Student: "Should I retake the SAT?"
Jenny: "Let's think strategically. Your 1530 is already strong - 97th percentile. The question is: will 20-30 more points meaningfully change your admissions chances? Probably not. Your time would be better spent on essays, which are far more differentiating."

Now, respond to this student in the same empathetic, strategic style:`;

What Online Learning Would Look Like:
// Proposed: Feedback Collection
class FeedbackCollector {
  async recordFeedback(
    conversationId: string,
    rating: number,      // 1-5 stars
    studentComment?: string,
    coachCorrection?: string
  ): Promise<void> {
    await db.insert('feedback', {
      conversation_id: conversationId,
      rating,
      student_comment: studentComment,
      coach_correction: coachCorrection,
      created_at: new Date()
    });
  }
}

// Proposed: Weekly Fine-tuning
class OnlineLearner {
  async updateModels(): Promise<void> {
    // 1. Collect last week's feedback
    const lowRatedConvos = await db.query(`
      SELECT c.*, f.rating, f.coach_correction
      FROM conversations c
      JOIN feedback f ON c.id = f.conversation_id
      WHERE f.rating <= 2  -- Poor ratings
      AND f.created_at > NOW() - INTERVAL '7 days'
    `);
    
    // 2. Generate training examples (original response + corrected version)
    const trainingExamples = lowRatedConvos.map(convo => ({
      messages: [
        { role: 'system', content: systemPrompt },
        { role: 'user', content: convo.user_message },
        { role: 'assistant', content: convo.coach_correction }  // Use corrected version
      ]
    }));
    
    // 3. Fine-tune model
    await openai.fineTuning.create({
      training_file: await this.uploadTrainingData(trainingExamples),
      model: 'gpt-4-0125-preview',
      suffix: `weekly-update-${new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0]}`
    });
    
    console.log(`Fine-tuned model with ${trainingExamples.length} corrections`);
  }
}

// File: backend/src/telemetry/tracer.ts (v3.2)

// 1. OpenTelemetry Tracing
const tracer = trace.getTracer('ivylevel');

function withSpan<T>(name: string, fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
  return tracer.startActiveSpan(name, async (span) => {
    try {
      const result = await fn();
      span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
      return result;
    } catch (error) {
      span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR, message: error.message });
      span.recordException(error);
      throw error;
    } finally {
      span.end();
    }
  });
}

// 2. Drift Detection
class DriftDetector {
  async checkDrift(): Promise<DriftReport> {
    // Compare last 7 days vs previous 7 days
    const recent = await this.getMetrics('7d');
    const baseline = await this.getMetrics('14d', offset='7d');
    
    const alerts = [];
    
    // Latency drift
    if (recent.avg_latency > baseline.avg_latency * 1.2) {
      alerts.push('⚠️ Latency increased 20%');
    }
    
    // Quality drift
    if (recent.user_satisfaction < baseline.user_satisfaction - 0.3) {
      alerts.push('⚠️ User satisfaction dropped');
    }
    
    // Cost drift
    if (recent.avg_cost_per_query > baseline.avg_cost_per_query * 1.3) {
      alerts.push('⚠️ Cost increased 30%');
    }
    
    return { alerts, recent, baseline };
  }
}

Pattern Definition: Autonomous iterative exploration of topics.
Match Assessment: ❌ NOT IMPLEMENTED
No deep research capabilities
Agents execute single queries, not iterative research
What It Would Look Like:
// User: "Research the best CS programs for robotics in the US"
// Deep Research Agent:
//   1. Search "top CS programs"
//   2. Search "robotics specializations"
//   3. Cross-reference: Which top CS programs have strong robotics?
//   4. Search each program: "MIT robotics research"
//   5. Synthesize: Rank programs by robotics fit
//   6. Return comprehensive report
Recommendation: LOW PRIORITY (Nice-to-have)

5.2 Critique Model
Pattern Definition: Specialized AI model to review/evaluate other AI outputs.
Match Assessment: ❌ NOT IMPLEMENTED
No critique model
Similar to Reflection pattern (which is also missing)
Recommendation: MEDIUM PRIORITY (Combine with Reflection)

Overall Maturity Score: 6.5/10
Strengths:
✅ World-class grounding (zero hallucination)
✅ Comprehensive tool use (105 tools)
✅ Strong memory system (4 layers)
✅ Multi-agent architecture (7 specialists)
Critical Gaps:
❌ No advanced reasoning (CoT, ToT, ReAct)
❌ No reflection/self-critique
❌ Limited planning (static, not dynamic)
❌ Weak HITL (no approval gates)
❌ No multimodal capabilities
Recommended Roadmap
Q1 2026 (High-Impact Quick Wins)
Add Chain of Thought to all agents (3 sprints)
Implement Reflection for Essay Agent (1 sprint)
Add Human-in-the-Loop approval gates (5 sprints)
Implement ReAct explicit reasoning (4 sprints)
Q2 2026 (Strategic Capabilities)
Add Multimodal support (PDF processing) (3 sprints)
Implement Dynamic Planning (7 sprints)
Add Tree of Thoughts for college list (3 sprints)
Fine-tune GPT-4-Jenny model (3 sprints)
Q3 2026 (Polish & Learn)
Add Procedural Memory (2 sprints)
Implement Online Learning pipeline (3 sprints)
Build Metrics Dashboard (2 sprints)
Add Drift Detection (1 sprint)
Total: ~40 sprints (20 months) to reach 9/10 maturity

CONCLUSION
IvyLevel has built a solid foundation with world-class grounding and tool use, but is missing critical reasoning patterns that would enable true agentic behavior. The platform is production-ready for its current scope (data-driven coaching), but needs advanced reasoning capabilities (CoT, ReAct, Reflection) to scale to more complex use cases (dynamic planning, essay critique, strategic decision-making).
Key Insight: IvyLevel has optimized for zero hallucination (100% success) at the expense of reasoning sophistication (30% completeness). This is the right trade-off for MVP, but the next phase requires adding reasoning while maintaining reliability.
Strategic Recommendation: Prioritize reasoning patterns (CoT, ReAct, Reflection) before adding new agents or features. These patterns will 3-5x the value of existing agents without requiring new tool development.
